# current.md — the live layer

**This file carries where the work stands, what is on the tables and shelves, what a next writer
must carry forward, and what is owed. It does not carry a list of things a phase did not do.
**It now carries only the live layer: the newest batch block, and the compact record of the three
stale re-dispatches of Batch 0002.** The superseded "after day 630" layer, the Batch 0002 and Batch
0003 blocks, and the two review-repair blocks are at
`state/archive/current.md.volume-13-batches-0001-to-0004-full.md`, word for word and undeleted, and
behind that at `state/archive/current.md.volume-12-close-through-volume-13-batch-0003.md` and
`state/archive/current.md.volumes-01-to-12.md`. A block here that says a thread stands is carrying
the live position forward; the reasoning behind it is in the archive and in the batch records.**

**THE FIGURES ARE IN THE BATCH RECORDS AND ARE NOT RESTATED HERE. For days 631 to 640 they are at
`state/batch-summaries/volume-13-batch-0004.md`; for days 621 to 630 at
`state/batch-summaries/volume-13-batch-0003.md`; for days 611 to 620 at
`state/batch-summaries/volume-13-batch-0002.md`. IF YOU ARE LOOKING FOR A FIGURE, CHECK THE ARCHIVE
AND THE BATCH RECORD BEFORE YOU RE-MEASURE IT, AND IF YOU RE-MEASURE IT, PUBLISH BOTH.**

**A NOTE ON THE WORD *ABOVE*, because the blocks below were written before this file was compacted
and each of them ends with a line saying it wins over every block above it: there are no blocks above
them here any more. *Above* now means the archives named in the header.**

## VOLUME 13 AFTER DAY 640 — CHAPTERS 0601 TO 0640 ON DISK, DAYS 601 TO 640, AND TEN DAYS OF THIS VOLUME STILL TO WRITE

**THE STORY, IN ONE PARAGRAPH, AND IT COMES FIRST BECAUSE THE COUNTS COME SECOND.** A man carried a board up a stair into a room and back down it, and a woman at the stall two floors below him lost a morning because he was standing at a bench with it against his own chest. A man washed a trough out along a row of flags with a rag that belongs to a stone lip and had it back on that lip before the light came off the flags, and a man at a salt wharf told two people at one end of that row to walk round the trough and told a third to carry her own crate, and was thanked by nobody and did not know the man who lifted the trough had opened the way for him. A woman told a room in about thirty words what a word in a column would cost and the cost was not hers and the man it was about did not thank her for saying it. A man got down on one knee at a stack of boards in a wharf yard and said in about nine words what that board is for, and a second line of grey grit lay across a figure nobody in that city has ever been asked about. A person walked eleven miles of flats with a slate under one arm and a tin in her two hands, stopped where a bank of silt had cut into a sill, and was not asked why she stopped. A man laid a plank across a wet sill and tied it with an old rope, and a man with a handcart went up that row and came back down it and walked on the plank and does not know whose hands laid it there. A board came off its two nails at the fourth hour and went back on them with a thin wedge under the near nail, and a bench was cleared and a sheet laid on it and nobody turned it. **Then a man took a piece of chalk out of an inside breast pocket it had not been out of since the two hundred and ninety-eighth day of this flood and wrote one word at the head of a column in front of about nine people while the woman who keeps that book stood at the near end of that bench and did not stop him, and nobody in that room read it out and nobody asked her why she did not.** The next day the same man named out loud what that had cost and it goes from the woman who keeps the book, and she wrote a word in the one place on a sheet with a pen and ink in her own hand and said that nobody asked her and that she did not choose it. And on the Thursday the wall of that room said one thing, and a man who trades nineteen rates on a board agreed with it out loud in about four seconds in nineteen words, was thanked by nobody, went down that stair and did his own trading at the far end of that city, and nobody in that room asked him about the space at the foot of the column between the other two on his own board.

**WHAT IS ON THE TABLES AND THE SHELVES AT THE END OF DAY 640, AND EVERY ITEM HOLDS.**

- A sheet with a heading and **one place** ruled under it is **shut** on the table under the one window of the room over a
  market, the bone still lying in the near fold of it, and **its one place is FILLED with a word that is neither a figure
  nor a person.** It came up that road on day 602, was read out on day 605 in the local language, and it goes back up
  that road on day 645 in a satchel with a flap. **No chapter of this volume prints that word or prints what that one
  place is a place of, and no chapter of this volume prints the word that went into the column on day 638.**
- The register holds **five** figures in the column that holds figures, in the order they were made, with a day written
  in beside each, **none struck, no sixth entered and none taken out**, and **the column on the right of that page is NO
  LONGER EMPTY. It carries one word near the head of that page, put there on day 638 in a hand that is not the keeper's,
  in front of about nine people, with the keeper standing at the near end of that bench and not asked. Nothing anywhere
  else on that page, and no second word on any day.** **There is nothing in this flood that can take a word back out of
  a place under a heading and leave that place bare, and after day 638 no page in this city can show that a place was
  ever left alone on purpose.**
- The second slate stands on the shelf behind that bench with a day cut across the head of it and nothing under it. It
  has never been picked up, never turned over and never written on, and **no hand but the keeper's went near that shelf
  on any of the forty days.**
- **The piece of chalk in the man of about thirty-four's own inside breast pocket came out of that pocket on day 638
  for the first time in three hundred and forty days, and it is back in the pocket the same afternoon.** The full form
  of day 298 is printed at full length in every chapter of this volume that carries it and is shortened to a relative
  phrase in none of them.
- The store's board is on its two nails with **three sets of figures**, none rubbed out, **not one of them carrying a
  line of words under it**, and no figure on it is printed. **No chapter of this volume says which of the three is the
  one anybody should be reading.** The wall above its top edge was washed on **none** of the fifty days and by no second
  person on any of them, and on day 637 a man had his own hand against the foot of that wall and his hand did not go
  above the top edge of that board.
- **THE TALLY-BOARD AT THE WHARF CARRIES A SECOND LINE OF GREY GRIT AS OF DAY 634**, lying across the first at an angle
  to it, and neither line was wiped and nobody has been told which board either came off, and the figure on that board
  is printed in no chapter of this volume. Day 642 is not one of this batch's days and stands to bring grit down onto a
  length of cord at that wharf.
- The store's own chalk is in a heap at the end of the market row with about a third of it in the channel, and on day
  632 the water out of a trough carried the edge of that heap over the flags into the channel and a woman put what was
  left into the box off her own fingers. Its box is on the step, the lid lies down on the rim, and **the lid does not
  shut**.
- The length of new rope over the back of its chair is never used, never cut, never taken up and never lifted, the man
  it belongs to is not in any of these ten chapters and is asked nothing in any of them, and **the coil of rope in the
  man of about sixty's handcart and the end of a rope that is not new on day 636 are a third rope and a fourth, and a
  chapter that names two ropes joins them in nothing.** Only one rope is named in Chapter 0636.
- There is **no eighth notice** in this flood and there is not going to be one. This batch wrote none.
- The count made on foot was made on **four** mornings, 511, 515, 517 and 521, **all four days named together in Chapter
  0635 and in no chapter of this volume printed without the day it was made in**, and the four do not agree and were not
  permitted to agree. **Day 635 was not one of those four mornings and nothing came off those flats on it.**
- A party of four is four, three went, one said no, and three barrows stand on a four-hundred-mile road nobody in this
  city can be shown one person on. **No chapter of this volume prints an elapsed figure for that road at any day.**
- The day on the door at the foot of that stair carries a date in full and **no name under it on any of the fifty
  days**, and a bare piece of door about as wide as a hand below the date. **The word going four hundred miles is the
  name of that room and it is on a sheet and not on that door.**

**THE THREE QUESTIONS OF DAYS 346, 396 AND 445, THE QUESTION OF DAY 498, THE QUESTION OF DAY 548 AND THE QUESTION OF
DAY 598 ALL STAND UNANSWERED. NONE HAS BEEN PUT IN A SECOND MOUTH BY ANY CHAPTER OF DAYS 631 TO 640. NO CHAPTER ASKS
THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-NINE WHAT THE MISSING NAME ON HIS OWN BOARD IS, AND NOTHING IN THOSE TEN CHAPTERS JOINS THE
SPACE ON THAT BOARD TO THE COLUMN ON THE RIGHT OF THAT PAGE IN ANY MOUTH OR IN ANY NARRATION.**

**THE DAY MAP. 601 Sunday, 650 Sunday, one chapter to one day, no double day. Bare-Month ordinals run 286 to 335 and no
chapter prints one outside that range, converts a count into a span, says the month is nearly over, or says what it is
a month of. Days 615 and 640 are the only days on which a Bare-Month ordinal and a day of this flood carry the same
figure, and no chapter may print both halves of either pair in one sentence, may join them, and may give either of them
a meaning. **625 and 626 are this volume's two middle days and neither may be called the midpoint in any chapter.** The
days the volume was built to reach, and which of them are written: 628 the name spoken — WRITTEN. 638 the column filled
— **WRITTEN**. 639 the one place filled — **WRITTEN**. 640 the wall's one panel — **WRITTEN**. 645 the sheet goes back
up the road, 648 the new question, 649 the register with its word, 650 the last image — none of them written yet.**

**THE NEXT PHASE IS `workspace/volume-13/batch-0005/PROMPT.md`, WRITING CHAPTERS 0641 TO 0650. IT CARRIES A COLUMN THAT
IS NO LONGER EMPTY AND A SHEET THAT IS SHUT AND HAS A WORD IN IT.**

6. The publication exposure in Volumes 01 to 04. Inherited, unpaid, and owed by a pass that says so.
7. **The disagreements inside the plan, ALL OF THEM, NONE RESOLVED BY ANYBODY.** The *party and the road* row against

## VOLUME 13, BATCH 0002 WAS RE-DISPATCHED THREE TIMES AFTER IT WAS COMPLETE. NOTHING MOVED. THIS IS THE COMPACT RECORD.

**Chapters 0611 to 0620 were written once, in one pass, and have never been rewritten, reworded,
reordered or restarted. The three re-dispatches each read the ten files against the ten cards, re-ran
the instruments, and appended a block to the four live state files and to the batch record. Net prose
written by the three: zero. The full text of all three blocks, word for word, is at
`state/archive/current.md.volume-13-batch-0002-stale-redispatch-full.md`, and the batch record's own
three blocks are at
`state/archive/batch-record-volume-13-batch-0002-full-3-verification-passes.md`. Nothing was deleted.**

**THE ONLY THINGS FROM THE THREE PASSES THAT A WRITER NEEDS, AND THEY ARE CORRECTIONS TO FIGURES
RATHER THAN EVENTS:**

- **Every one of the ten cards is carried by its chapter, each card's one bolded voice is alone in the
  bold marks of its chapter, and the day map's Adrian column holds exactly: Adrian Vale is on a page
  in 0611 and in 0615 and in no other of the eight.**
- **The batch record's §6 published a false zero on the strict sentence-scale row.** It reads
  `strict = 0, in 0 files`; the corrected figure is **six whole-sentence reproductions of nine words or
  more, in three of the ten files.** A published zero that is not zero is the same failure as a wrong
  count. The corrected figure and the class it belongs to are at
  `state/batch-summaries/volume-13-batch-0002.md`, in the compacted block at the foot of that file.
- **A required declaration of an object row is not a duplication.** Four of the six are that, and two
  are this manuscript's own non-agreement formula, which is a motif and not a finding.
- **The descriptor map is seven distinct descriptors in fifty-seven occurrences, against the record's
  seven in fifty-eight. Both stand.** No new descriptor was taken and the pool of clean candidates for
  a new person is still **ZERO**.
- **The marker fault is zero in all ten files on both readings. The word-run pass at forty words is
  zero in all ten at all three scopes. The day run is ten rows and zero mismatches on
  chapter-equals-day, the weekday and the Bare-Month ordinal at once. The `>` panel count is zero in
  all ten.** The object inventory's second column holds on every row these ten touch.
- **Three instrument faults were caught in the passes' own instruments and not on any page** — a
  standalone em dash counted as a token, and a weekday list missing Monday that stopped the day run
  on day 616 after five correct rows. `outline/volume-13.md` §19.6 requires an instrument that cannot
  read a compound to report its own fault, and that is what happened.

**THE POINTER THAT A LATER PASS FOUND STALE AND THAT IS NOW CORRECT: Batch 0002's ten cards are NOT at
the head of this file. This file carries the live layer only. They are in
`state/archive/current.md.volume-12-close-through-volume-13-batch-0003.md` under their own heading, and
that statement in `outline/batches/volume-13-batch-0003.md` has been corrected to point here.**

**THE DEBTS ARE UNCHANGED AND UNPAID.** The eleven inherited debts and the six named at
`outline/volume-13.md` §21.1; the false zero of two words in four published batch records of Volume 12;
the narrator-frame family, which stands on the strict reading and on the looser one; the publication
exposure in Volumes 01 to 04; and the plan's own internal disagreements — the *party and the road* row
against its own escalation, the day entry for a box of chalk's lid against its own object row, the
Adrian column against its own §4.1 table, and the *"the string"* in that §4.1 row for day 601, which is
in no chapter of Batch 0001. `chapters/volume-10/chapter-0475.md` is on disk and is a legitimate
review-repair of a gap in a finished volume, published as such in `state/chapter-summaries.md`.

**AND THE ONE FINDING A WRITER OR A FIX PASS MAY NOT ACT ON, BECAUSE IT IS NOT OURS.**
`state/phase-ledger.json` reads `phase-000-bootstrap` with the repository thirteen volumes and 640
chapters deep, and no script in `scripts/` touches that file, so nothing will ever advance it. It is
controller state, it is reported here, it was not opened, and **no agent may fix it.** The
`workspace/volume-13/batch-0002/` and `workspace/volume-13/batch-0004/` marker files — `.done`,
`.attempts`, `.deferred`, `.retry-after`, `.wip-conflict` — are dispatcher state written by
`scripts/novel_runner.sh`, they are the reason this batch was re-dispatched three times, and they are
not ours to create or clear either. **Both are controller matters and are named here so that a human
who owns the dispatcher has them in one place.**

## VOLUME 13 AFTER DAY 640 — VERIFICATION PASS ON CHAPTERS 0631 TO 0640, AND WHAT A NEXT WRITER CARRIES

**THIS BLOCK IS AT THE FOOT OF THIS FILE AND IT WINS OVER EVERY BLOCK ABOVE IT. NOTHING ABOVE IS EDITED.**

**THE STORY, AND IT HAS NOT MOVED.** A man carried a board up a stair into a room and back down it. A man washed
the outside of a trough out along a row of flags and had the rag back on its stone lip before the light came off the
flags. A woman told a room in about thirty words what a word in a column would cost, and the cost was not hers. A
man got down on one knee at a stack of boards and said in nine words what that surface is for, over a figure two
lines of grey grit now lie across and nobody in that city has ever been asked about. A person walked eleven miles of
flats with a slate under one arm and a tin in her two hands and stopped where the silt had cut into a sill, and
nobody asked her why. A man laid a plank across a wet sill and tied it with a rope that is not new, and a man with
a handcart walked on it and does not know whose hands laid it. A board came off two nails and went back on them with
a wedge under the near nail. **Then a man took a piece of chalk out of an inside breast pocket it had not been out
of since the two hundred and ninety-eighth day of this flood and wrote one word at the head of a column in front of
about nine people, and the woman who keeps that book stood at the near end of that bench and did not stop him and
was not asked why she did not. The next day she wrote a word in the one place on that sheet with a pen and ink and
named out loud what all of it costs and who it goes from, and the sheet was shut on the same table on the day after
that. On the third day the wall of that room said one thing and the man of about thirty-nine agreed with it out loud
in about four seconds and went down the stair.** All of that was on disk before this pass and all of it is on disk
after it.

**WHAT THIS PASS CHANGED, AND IT IS THREE THINGS.** Chapter 0634 now prints **a Friday**, which is its day, and it
was the only one of the ten that printed no weekday. Two over-long paragraphs in Chapter 0636 were split at their
own joints — **at 166 words and at 127 words** — and not one fact, count, hour, object or card requirement changed
in either. A nineteen-word self-repetition in Chapter 0640 was broken. **NO CHAPTER WAS RESTARTED, REWORDED
OUTSIDE THOSE THREE PLACES, REORDERED OR SHORTENED, AND NO CARD, DAY, ORDINAL, ANCHOR, FORM, NOTICE, OBJECT COUNT,
CAST MEMBER OR DECISION OF RECORD MOVED.**

## VOLUME 13, BATCH 0005 — CHAPTERS 0641 TO 0650, DAYS 641 TO 650. THIS BLOCK IS NEWEST AND IT WINS OVER EVERY BLOCK ABOVE IT. NOTHING ABOVE IS EDITED.

**THE STORY, IN ONE PARAGRAPH, AND THE STORY COMES FIRST.** A woman at the near end of a bench named out loud, in her own mouth, in front of about nine people, what it is to be the one everybody in a room has been looking at instead of the book, and a man at the far end of that bench disagreed with her for a reason of his own and was not argued into agreement, and a woman with a hand-basket stood at the one window from the eighth hour until the light went off it and went back down that stair with what she came up it for still inside the basket. **A man at a salt wharf took a length of cord up off the boards, laid it on the top of a stack, and grit came off the boards above onto the cord and stayed on the cord and did not come off that board, and nobody wiped it, and the cord went back down on the same boards with a line of grey grit along it.** A man of about thirty-four walked out past the last named house with a board under his arm, stood at a fence of sixteen willow posts and eleven withies, put his own hand on the top of the near post, and came back without measuring it and without moving a post, and nobody in that room could have asked him about the board or the pocket and nobody did. A room asked one question all morning and the only person in it who could have answered was not asked. **A woman folded a sheet shut along the two folds it came up that road in and pressed the bone down the whole length of each with the heel of her own hand, and a man with a satchel with a flap took it down that stair and it went four hundred miles and is not coming back, and nobody in that room opened it.** A board was washed once and not twice and a man two stalls along leaned past it and went away with a mark on his own hand. A man went down a road with a closed flap to a yard that is not the yard he went to and came back up it. **A man asked one woman one question about a room and she did not answer it and the room did not hear it.** A woman took a book off a shelf and looked down the column beside five figures and put it back where it was. **And on the last morning a board was washed and went back up, a man looked at a fence, a board at a wharf lay face up under grey grit nobody wiped, a book stood on a shelf with one word near the head of the column beside five figures, three barrows stood on a four-hundred-mile road, and a man walked the length of a market row with a board under his arm and nobody in that row stopped him.**

**WHAT IS ON THE TABLES AND THE SHELVES AT THE END OF DAY 650, AND EVERY ITEM HOLDS.**

- **THE FOURTH ONE-PLACE FORM IS OUT OF THIS CITY.** A sheet with a heading and **one place** ruled under it had that one place filled on day 639 with a word that is neither a figure nor a person, was shut on that table on day 640, and **went back up the four-hundred-mile road on day 645 in a satchel with a flap** carried by the man of about thirty-one who carries things for a living and who has never opened anything he has carried in this flood. It is the second thing this flood has sent out of this city with words on it, it is not withdrawn, and **it is not coming back, and no chapter of days 641 to 650 put it on a table in this city, and nobody in this flood can bring it back.** **No chapter of this volume prints that word and no chapter prints what that one place is a place of.**
- The register holds **five** figures in the column that holds figures, in the order they were made, with a day written in beside each, **none struck, no sixth entered and none taken out**, and the column on the right of that page **carries one word near the head of it, put there on day 638 in a hand that is not the keeper's, and nothing anywhere else on that page, and no second word on any day of days 641 to 650.**
- The second slate stands on the shelf behind that bench with a day cut across the head of it and nothing under it. It was not picked up, not turned over and not written on on any of these ten days, and **no hand but the keeper's went near that shelf on any of them.**
- The piece of chalk in the man of about thirty-four's own inside breast pocket **came out on day 638 and is back in that pocket, and it was in that pocket at every hour of day 650 and it was not out of it on any of these ten days.** The full form of day 298 is printed at full length in the chapters that carry it and is shortened to a relative phrase in none of them, and **no chapter of these ten prints what is on his thumb.**
- The store's board is on its two nails with **three sets of figures**, none rubbed out, **not one of them carrying a line of words under it**, and no figure on it is printed. **No chapter of these ten says which of the three is the one anybody should be reading.** **The wall above its top edge was washed by nobody on any of these ten days and no hand went above that top edge on any of them**, and Chapter 0646 puts the flat of a hand on the **lower** edge of that board and nowhere else.
- **THE TALLY-BOARD AT THE WHARF CARRIES TWO LINES OF GREY GRIT ON DAY 642, one of them coming down onto a length of cord and NOT off the board, and neither line was wiped.** Its figure is printed in no chapter of this volume, no chapter says where it came from, nobody in this volume asks him about it, and that board is not turned face down on any of these ten days and cannot be. About four people a day walk past the open end of that yard and **no chapter of these ten takes their number.**
- The store's own chalk is in a heap at the end of the market row with about a third of it in the channel, its box is on the step, the lid lies down on the rim, **the lid does not shut**, and a thumb's width of grit is under the near side of that rim. **Nobody in this city is going to be told about it.**
- **The length of new rope over the back of its chair is never used, never cut, never taken up and never lifted on any of these ten days, the man it belongs to is in none of these ten chapters and is asked nothing in any of them, and the coil of rope in the man of about sixty's handcart and the end of a rope that is not new on day 636 are a third rope and a fourth.**
- **There is no eighth notice in this flood and this batch wrote none.**
- The count made on foot was made on **four** mornings, 511, 515, 517 and 521, and **this volume makes no fifth morning and takes no fifth figure out**, and Chapter 0642 prints no figure of that count at all and names no morning of it.
- A party of four is four, three went, one said no, and three barrows stand on a four-hundred-mile road nobody in this city can be shown one person on. **No chapter of these ten prints an elapsed figure for that road at any day and no chapter prints a days-out figure at any day.**
- The day on the door at the foot of that stair carries a date in full and **no name under it on any of these ten days**, and a bare piece of door about as wide as a hand below the date. **The word that went four hundred miles is the name of that room and it is on a sheet and not on that door, and no chapter of these ten put it there.**

**THE DAY MAP. 601 Sunday, 650 Sunday, one chapter to one day, no double day. Bare-Month ordinals run 286 to 335 and no chapter of this volume prints one outside that range. Days 615 and 640 are the only days on which a Bare-Month ordinal and a day of this flood carry the same figure, and neither pair was joined or given a meaning by any chapter. 625 and 626 are this volume's two middle days and no chapter of this batch called either of them the midpoint. The days the volume was built to reach, and which of them are written: 628 the name spoken — WRITTEN. 638 the column filled — WRITTEN. 639 the one place filled — WRITTEN. 640 the wall's one panel — WRITTEN. 645 the sheet goes back up that road — **WRITTEN, ON THE DAY THE PLAN FIXED IT**. 648 the new question — **WRITTEN, ASKED TO ONE WOMAN AND NOT ANSWERED**. 649 the register with its word — WRITTEN. 650 the last image — WRITTEN.**

**THE VOLUME IS COMPLETE AT FIFTY CHAPTERS AND FIFTY DAYS, DAYS 601 TO 650, AND NO CHAPTER PAST 0650 EXISTS.**

## VOLUME 13 CLOSE — CHAPTERS 0601 TO 0650, DAYS 601 TO 650. THIS BLOCK IS NEWEST AND IT WINS OVER EVERY BLOCK ABOVE IT. NOTHING ABOVE IS EDITED.

**THE VOLUME IS COMPLETE. FIFTY CHAPTERS ON FIFTY DAYS, DAYS 601 TO 650, ONE CHAPTER TO ONE DAY, NO DOUBLE DAY, AND NO CHAPTER PAST 0650 EXISTS ON DISK. THIS BLOCK RECORDS THE VOLUME AND WRITES NO PROSE, REPAIRS NO CHAPTER AND STARTS NO SIXTH BATCH.** The close record is `state/volume-13-close.md` and it is the page a writer of the next volume reads before planning anything.

**WHAT THE FIFTY DAYS WERE, IN ONE PARAGRAPH, AND THE STORY COMES FIRST.** A sheet with a heading and one place ruled under it came up the quarterly round on day 602 with nothing whatever in that one place, and about nine people who were already in a room over a market looked at it for four hours and could not say what that one place was for. A man with a piece of chalk he had not used in three hundred and six days said so in about nine words and was thanked by nobody. On the Thursday the woman who keeps that city's only public book read the sheet out in the local language and said what it was for before anybody asked. **On the nearer of the two middle days the same man said in about eleven words that the word which goes in the column on the right of that page is his and goes in before the word which goes in the one place on the sheet, and that neither of the two is to be read out, and the woman who keeps the book was at the near end of that bench and was not asked one thing about either of them.** On day 628 she asked him one question about him and he said one thing out loud and it went nowhere. **On day 638 his chalk came out of that pocket for the first time since day 298 and one word went into the column on the right of that page in a hand that is not the keeper's, with about nine people in the room and the keeper standing at the near end of the bench, and nobody asked her.** On day 639 the second cost was named out loud and a word went into the one place on the sheet in her own hand. On day 640 the wall said one thing in fifty chapters and the man who trades nineteen rates on a board agreed with it in about four seconds and paid for it in the space about two fingers wide at the foot of the column between the other two, and nobody asked him about that space. **On day 641 the woman it is about said out loud that she was the only one in that room who could have said what that column was for and that nobody had asked her, and nothing in that room was done about it at any hour of that day. On day 645 the sheet went back up a four-hundred-mile road in a satchel with a flap and is not coming back. On day 648 the man who carries things asked her one question about a room and she did not answer it and the room did not hear it. On day 649 she took the book off its shelf, looked down the column, and put it back. And on day 650 a board was washed once and went back up on its two nails, a wall above it was not washed, a book stood on a shelf with five figures in one column and one word near the head of the column beside them, a board at a wharf lay face up under unwiped grit, a length of new rope lay over the back of a chair, three barrows stood on a four-hundred-mile road, and a man walked the length of a market row with a board under his arm and nobody in that row stopped him.**

**WHAT STANDS AT THE END OF DAY 650, AND EVERY ITEM HOLDS.** The fourth one-place form is four hundred miles away and not coming back. The register holds five figures in the column that holds figures, none struck, no sixth entered and none taken out, and the column on the right carries one word near the head of that page and nothing anywhere else on it. The second slate is on its shelf with a day across the head of it and nothing under it, and no hand but the keeper's went near that shelf on any of the fifty days. The chalk is in the man of about thirty-four's own inside breast pocket. The store's board is on its two nails with three sets of figures and not one carrying a line of words under it, and the wall above its top edge was washed by nobody. The tally-board at the wharf carries two lines of grey grit and its figure is printed in no chapter of this volume and nobody in this volume asks the man of about thirty-eight one thing about it. The box of chalk on the step has a lid that does not shut. The length of new rope is untouched. There is no eighth notice. The count made on foot was made on four mornings and this volume makes no fifth. A party of four is four, three went, one said no, and no chapter of this volume prints an elapsed figure for that road at any day. The day on the door carries a date in full and no name under it on any of the fifty days.

**THE FIGURES ARE IN THE CLOSE RECORD AND ARE NOT RESTATED HERE.** The word-run check, the sentence-scale pass, the paragraph composition, the recurring figures, the Bare-Month form, the object inventory's third column, the elapsed counts, the descriptor map and the Adrian column are all at `state/volume-13-close.md` §6 to §10, each with its instrument and its file count.

**THE THREE THINGS A WRITER OF THE NEXT VOLUME MUST CARRY, AND NONE OF THEM IS SETTLED.** **THE DAYS PAST 650 ARE UNPLANNED GROUND UNTIL A PHASE WRITES THE PLAN FOR THE VOLUME THAT BEGINS AT CHAPTER 0651, AND THAT PHASE HAS NOT RUN.** A place on a page that was left alone on purpose is no longer a place on a page that was left alone on purpose, and the only person who could have said which of the two a given page is was standing in a room on day 644 and was not asked. The question of day 648 is asked and unanswered and may be asked again in a mouth, in daylight, in a room, with a date on the door, and may not be answered.

**THE DEBTS, CARRIED WHOLE AND UNPAID.** The eleven inherited debts and the six named at `outline/volume-13.md` §21.1; the four missing card files of Volume 12; the false zero of two words in four published batch records of Volume 12, not reprinted and not repaired here; the narrator-frame family, at **zero occurrences in zero files on Reading A and at 898 occurrences in fifty files on Reading B**; the publication exposure in Volumes 01 to 04; **the seven forty-word runs inside this volume, in eight of its fifty files, longest fifty-two words — corrected from a first edition of this record that published thirteen at eighty to ninety-two, and the correction is at `state/volume-13-close.md` §7.1a — that a published after-figure of zero does not come back on**; the one of the eleven that Chapter 0608 does not carry him in; and **the seven disagreements inside and around the plan, all carried and resolved by nobody.** `state/phase-ledger.json` reads `phase-000-bootstrap`; it is controller state, it is reported here, and it was not opened.

# VOLUME 14, DAYS 651 TO 700 — SET BY THE VOLUME 14 OUTLINE PHASE. THE PLAN IS `outline/volume-14.md` AND EVERY FIGURE BELOW IS POINTED AT AND NOT RESTATED, because a state file is a record of a plan and the plan is the plan.

**THE STORY FIRST, AND IT IS FOUR SENTENCES.** A book in this city has a word in one of its columns in a hand that is not the hand that keeps it, and the man whose hand it is is two stalls along and has not been asked. A door at the foot of the stair at the back of the room over a market carries a date in chalk and a bare piece of board about as wide as a hand under that date, and in six volumes nobody has put a name there and nobody has said whether anybody may. Both of those are the same thing: a surface in a public place that a person in a room can write on, and the only instrument this book has for finding out who did it is a room, and a room cannot be used on a page without telling the room. **On day 675 a room over a market decides out loud that the piece of board is not to be written on, and the decision is written nowhere, and from that morning the board means nothing at all, and that is the thing the volume spends, and the person it costs is the man who decided and the woman who is not asked.**

**WHAT A WRITER OF VOLUME 14 NEEDS AND WHERE IT IS, IN ONE PLACE, WITH NOTHING RESTATED.** The plan is `outline/volume-14.md`. The day map, the pressure column and the Adrian column are at its §14.3 and are part of the map and not a note on it. The eleven wants, the thing his hands are on and whether he got it are at its §4.1 and a card may only take them. The thing the volume spends and whose it is is at its §6.5. The first decision, its day and its seven words are at its §6.6. The wall's one day and the fact that nobody agrees with it are at its §6.7. The word column of twenty-four zeros that is going to stay zero is at its §6.8 and its §19.3. The four things this manuscript has never had on a page are at its §19.4. The mid-volume decision is at its §8. The climax is at its §10, the resolution at its §11, the finding at its §12, and the new question at its §13. The calendar is `bible/power-system.md` §61, a new numbered section, and it amends nothing. The anchors and the twenty round-figure trap days and the two compounded days are at its §14.5. The prohibitions are at its §16 at their length, the sixteen of them, fifteen inherited and one new. The prose instructions are at its §17, the twenty of them. The object inventory in both columns and the per-chapter column are at its §20.6. The eight frames and the word-run rule are at its §20.7. The recurring figures with both case flags are at its §20.3. The checks with both runs of every pass in the same row are at its §20. The closing shapes and the five this volume puts off limits for itself are at its §20.9.

**THE FOUR THINGS A WRITER OF VOLUME 14 MUST CARRY, AND NONE OF THEM IS SETTLED.** **THE DAYS PAST 650 ARE NO LONGER UNPLANNED GROUND: THEY ARE PLANNED, AND THE PLAN IS `outline/volume-14.md`, AND NO CHAPTER OF VOLUME 14 EXISTS.** The volume is fifty chapters on fifty days, one chapter to one day, day 651 a Monday and the three hundred and thirty-sixth day of the Bare Month, day 700 a Monday and the three hundred and eighty-fifth, the two ends the same weekday because 700 − 651 = 49 = 7 × 7, and that is arithmetic and not a symmetry anybody may use. **THE REGISTER'S RIGHT-HAND COLUMN IS SPENT AND NO CHAPTER OF VOLUME 14 MAY PUT A SECOND WORD IN IT. THE DOOR'S BARE PIECE OF BOARD GETS NOTHING PUT UNDER IT ON ANY OF THE FIFTY DAYS AND NO CHAPTER MAY PUT ANYTHING THERE. THE WALL SAYS ONE THING AND IT IS ON ONE DAY AND IT IS NOT ON ANY OF THE OTHER FORTY-NINE, AND ON THAT DAY NOBODY IN THE ROOM AGREES WITH IT. THE FOUR QUESTIONS OF DAYS 346, 396 AND 445 AND THE QUESTIONS OF DAYS 498, 548, 598 AND 648 STAND UNANSWERED, AND A QUESTION IS ASKED AGAIN ON DAY 698 IN A DIFFERENT DIRECTION AND IS NOT ANSWERED, AND THE WOMAN OF ABOUT SIXTY-NINE DOES NOT ASK A FOURTH FOR THE SEVENTH VOLUME RUNNING.** And the one finding this phase publishes that is about a person and not a figure: **THE STALLKEEPER OF ABOUT THIRTY-FOUR IS TWO PEOPLE IN THIS REPOSITORY AND NO PAGE HAS SAID SO** — the plan and the pages behind this volume say *man*, the state layer and two close records say *woman*, and the plan's §16.16 forbids any chapter of Volume 14 from settling it, and this block does not settle it either.

**THE DEBTS, CARRIED WHOLE AND UNPAID, AND ONE NEW FACT ABOUT ONE OF THEM.** The eleven inherited debts at `outline/volume-14.md` §21.2; the six at its §21.1; the four missing card files of Volume 12; the false zero of two words in four published batch records of Volume 12; the narrator-frame family; the publication exposure in Volumes 01 to 04; **and `chapters/volume-10/chapter-0475.md`, WHICH IS ON DISK. **WHAT WAS WRONG AND WHAT IT IS NOW, IN ONE LINE EACH, AND NOTHING BELOW IS A NEW DECISION.** (1) The pressure rotation at `outline/volume-14.md` §15 listed forty-eight chapters and printed a sum of fifty on a table that did not carry it; **the character row is fifteen, the table carries fifty distinct chapters, and every one of the fifty map days is in exactly one row.** (2) The day map's Adrian column carried ten marks where §4.1, this file, `state/continuity.md`, `state/character-state.md` and `outline/series.md` all carry eleven; **day 0665 is marked and the column is eleven and agrees with §4.1 row for row.** (3) Three files attributed a corpus figure of four hundred and ninety-nine to a plan that publishes five hundred and ninety-nine; **the figure is corrected at all three and the real inherited 599-against-499 disagreement is published and not repaired.** (4) §17.9 said a word stood in a column *on every page*; **it is one page, near the head of it, and the column is spent.** (5) §7.3 licensed two chalk days; **there is one, and §6.6 and §9 item 28 are the same day.** (6) §14.5 said the anchor table was not reprinted and then reprinted thirty-seven of forty rows, three of which its own trap list cites; **the reprint and the authority now agree row for row at forty, every value is a subtraction from its own row, and day 645 has one name.** (7) The batch prompt had no batch summary in its output list and named no successor; **both are now in it.** (8) Two trap rows had no reason; **both have one and the twenty-day list is unchanged and reproduces.** (9) A second handle lost a coil of rope, a cast list lost three people, and one anchor cell said two figures where it prints four; **all three are back.**

**AND WHAT A WRITER OF BATCH 0001 SHOULD TAKE FROM THIS BLOCK, WHICH IS THE WHOLE OF WHAT A REPAIR OWES THE NEXT PHASE.** **THE ROTATION IS 9 / 15 / 9 / 6 / 6 / 3 / 2 AND SUMS TO FIFTY, and the character row is the largest row in the volume, and its fifteen chapters are the ones where a chapter about a person can be written with no bolded turn in it at all.** **ADRIAN IS IN ELEVEN CHAPTERS AND 0665 IS ONE OF THEM**, with his want published at §4.1 and unchanged by this repair. | 0652 | 652 | Tuesday | the three hundred and thirty-seventh | political | — | a man of about thirty-nine says out loud that nineteen rates are things two people agreed on and that he cannot name one person who agreed on the thing at the head of that column, and the man of about thirty-one tells him so at the ninth hour before he has to work it out |

## What is on the objects on day 660, with the counts that hold

One trough, one bucket, one rag, and the rag is on the stone lip of the step in every chapter that places it. One store's board with three sets of figures, none rubbed out, and not one carrying a line of words under it. One wall above the top edge of that board, washed once in the whole of this flood by the man who washes that board and by no second person. One register with five figures in the middle column and a day beside each and no sixth, and one word near the head of the column on the right and nothing anywhere else on that page. One second slate on that shelf, not picked up, not turned over, not written on, at any hour of any of these ten. Seven documents: three of four places and four of one place, none joined, none in one hand, none compared out loud. Seven notices and no eighth. Sixteen willow posts and eleven withies, not measured, no post moved. One length of new rope over the back of the chair at the far end of that bench, unused, not lifted, not cut, not taken up. A date in chalk on the outside of the door at the foot of that stair with nothing under it on any of these ten days and a bare piece of door about as wide as a hand below it. **THE FIRST EDITION OF THIS BLOCK PRINTED THIS SAME LIST WITHOUT THE WORD ONE IN FRONT OF THE BUCKET, AND A REVIEW FOUND TWO CHAPTERS WITH A SECOND BUCKET IN THEM, WHICH IS A COUNT THAT HOLDS AND MAY NOT DIFFER, AND BOTH CHAPTERS WERE REPAIRED AND THE LIST IS PUBLISHED HERE AS ONE OF EACH.**

## The figures this batch stands on, with the reading on the same line

Every pass was run twice, once in file order and once in reverse order with the index rebuilt, and both numbers are on the same row in `state/batch-summaries/volume-14-batch-0001.md`. **On these ten files as they now stand: the bolded share is 4.61 to 13.33 with a weighted mean of 8.49 over fourteen thousand five hundred and sixty-two words; one hundred and ninety-nine prose paragraphs of which thirty carry a quotation mark and eighteen carry a bold mark and NONE carries a bold mark without a quotation mark; twenty-two of the one-sentence paragraphs are marked speech, sixteen are speech-attribution lead-ins on Reading A and seventy-two are free-standing; twenty-eight paragraphs of three or more sentences and TWENTY-SEVEN OF THOSE CARRY A PHYSICAL ACTION AND EVERY ONE OF THE TEN CHAPTERS HAS AT LEAST ONE; the panel count is zero; the twenty-four-word zero column is at zero; and the days, the weekdays and the Bare-Month ordinals resolve on all ten in both runs.** The range sits inside the range the plan measured on the fifty files behind this one, which is 2.79 to 17.16 at an unweighted mean of 8.06 and a weighted mean of 7.95. **The mean is a consequence and not a plan and no reviewer may fail this batch on it.**

## What is deliberately still zero, and what is not being claimed about it

No new person. The descriptor instrument returns five distinct descriptors across the ten files and forty occurrences, and **every one of the five is a person who already had one and a page: *man of about thirty-four* who keeps a stall at fourteen, *man of about twenty-seven* who washes that board at nine, *man of about thirty-nine* who trades on a board at seven, *woman of about twenty-nine* who keeps a public register at seven, and *man of about thirty-one* who carries things for a living at three. *Man of about thirty-seven* is at ZERO and was cut out of Chapter 0655 on the first run and is not back.** **THE FIRST EDITION OF THIS BLOCK PRINTED FOUR DESCRIPTORS AT THIRTY-THREE OCCURRENCES, WHICH WAS WRONG IN TWO CELLS OF FOUR, AND THE FIGURE THAT REPRODUCED — the total of thirty-three — REPRODUCED WHILE TWO OF ITS PARTS DID NOT, WHICH IS THE SAME KIND OF FIGURE AS ONE THAT REPRODUCED ON NONE. THE DESCRIPTOR MAP COUNTS OCCURRENCES AND DOES NOT DECIDE WHICH OCCURRENCE IS THE SAME PERSON, AND THE SECOND HALF OF THAT SENTENCE IS WHY THE FIRST EDITION OF THIS BLOCK WAS WRONG: IT TITLED A HEAD BY WHAT A PERSON DOES AND NOT BY WHO THEY ARE, AND A REVIEW FOUND THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-FOUR WHO CARRIES THINGS FOR A LIVING PRINTED IN TWO CHAPTERS AGAIN INSTEAD OF THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-ONE, WHICH PUT TWO MEN OF ABOUT THIRTY-FOUR IN ONE ROOM OVER A MARKET. BOTH WERE CUT AND THE PASS WAS RUN AGAIN.** The pool for a new person is zero by the reading and this batch added nobody, and that is the fifth volume running, and a decision not to do a thing is a decision and a reader in a year cannot tell one from an oversight. The twenty-four words the plan published at zero are at zero on these ten. The words *passage* and *privilege* are at zero. There is no bare three-digit numeral in any of the ten. There is no room count for anybody and no *dozen*. **The stair at the back of that room is NOT said to be nine steps on any of these ten days and the one permission that carries is one chapter of the fifty and not one sentence, and this batch did not spend it.** The wall said nothing on all ten days and the panel count is zero and no `>` block stands in any of these ten files.

## What this batch decided, and what it did not decide

It decided nothing. It invented no person, took no descriptor, wrote no notice, put nothing under the date on that door on any of these ten days, did not put a word in the column on the right, did not take the piece of chalk out of that pocket on any of these ten days, did not trace the mark, did not measure the fence, did not move a post, did not use the length of new rope, did not count the days since the fourth of the four said no at any value, did not open the satchel, did not pick up the second slate, did not read out the sheet on the shelf, and did not name the missing name on the man of about thirty-nine's board. **THE FIRST EDITION OF THIS BLOCK NAMED CHANDBERS 0651 TO 0660 AS A WALL THAT SAID NOTHING ON ALL TEN DAYS AND THAT STAND. IT ALSO SAID, OF THE SAME TEN, THAT EACH PRINTS ITS OWN DAY'S DATE ON THAT DOOR EXCEPT ONE, AND THAT WAS WRONG: FOUR OF THE TEN PRINT IT AND SIX OF THE TEN NEVER GO NEAR THAT DOOR, WHICH IS WHAT THE PLAN'S OWN PER-CHAPTER COLUMN FOR THOSE SIX SAYS. THE ERROR WAS IN THIS BLOCK AND NOT IN A CHAPTER AND IT IS CORRECTED IN THE CONTINUITY BLOCK AT `state/continuity.md`.** No day, no weekday, no Bare-Month ordinal, no anchor, no form, no notice, no object count that holds, no cast member and no decision of record moved, and no plan file was edited. `outline/ending.md` was not opened, this volume introduces no new final enemy and no new world, and the planned ending is untouched. ## VOLUME 14 BATCH 0002 — CHAPTERS 0661 TO 0670 ON DISK, DAYS 661 TO 670

**The story, in one paragraph, and it comes first.** A wharf yard with a board under two lines of grit gets a thumb put to it and keeps the grit, and a stack sits crooked and is set right, and a barrow rolls and is caught. A board comes down off its nails and goes back up while the wall above it stays out of it, and a room puts the two side by side and finds they do not agree. Four people mean four things by whether a page may be written on and no two are wrong, and a keeper says she cannot show which of two same pages hers is. A satchel is shown shut and put back shut, and a board is carried the length of a yard and set on the wrong stack, and a knife is wiped and put back uncut while two men name what they carry. A board comes down and goes up with nothing decided, a question goes round a room four times and is answered four times with not-knowing and is not put to the keeper, a keeper spends a morning alone with two words she will never speak, and a stall keeps its face turned in while the row walks past without counting.

**Figures, forward run and reverse run with index rebuilt, both on the same row, instrument: whole file on whitespace, heading and rules left in, bold spans counted.** **0661: 1162 words, 77 bold, 6.63 — AND THAT CELL WAS WRONG. Re-measured from `git show HEAD:` on the same instrument it is 1159 words and 6.64, and the ten total 8875 and not 8878.** The other nine cells reproduce and the bolded column reproduces at 769. The correction is at §4 of `state/batch-summaries/volume-14-batch-0002.md` with both figures on the row. 0662: 970, 83, 8.56. 0663: 1100, 85, 7.73. 0664: 898, 83, 9.24. 0665: 897, 74, 8.25. 0666: 724, 79, 10.91. 0667: 766, 71, 9.27. 0668: 802, 82, 10.22. 0669: 819, 69, 8.42. 0670: 740, 66, 8.92. Total 8878 as published and **8875** as measured, / 769 / 8.66 weighted, both runs agree. Range 6.63 to 10.91. The mean is a consequence and not a plan.

**Markers, both runs agree.** Prose paragraphs 98; speech paragraphs 20; bold paragraphs 20, every one carrying a quotation mark and every one on its chapter's carded voice, twice in all ten chapters; bold-without-quotation 0; panels 0; longest consecutive speech run 1 against a ceiling of 4; no exchange carries two bold marks. Paragraphs of three or more sentences: 35, of which 32 carry a physical action read and not swept; every chapter has at least one. One-sentence paragraphs: 32 free-standing narration beats, 0 one-sentence marked speeches standing alone outside sections, all legal in the three counted-apart classes. First-person-restricted reading 0 of 0.

**Days and forms, both runs agree.** Ten distinct days 661 to 670, no gap, no double. Weekdays Thursday to Saturday re-derived from day 1 Tuesday. Bare-Month ordinals 346th to 355th, one per chapter, all resolving to day less 315; month's name in 10 of 10. No ordinal after the 385th, no ordinal for a month, no span, and the words length, long and short applied to that month are at zero. Round-figure trap days 663, 664, 665, 668, 670 print none of their listed figures. Same-figure trap: no sentence carries both halves of any pair. No elapsed figure for the four-hundred-mile road at any day. No counting of the days since the fourth of the four said no. Digits in prose 0; the stair is never said to be nine steps; passage and privilege at zero; man of about thirty-seven at zero; rooms and dozen at zero; no new descriptor; the stallholder is the man of about thirty-four in all occurrences and is never in one room with any other of that age and trade.

**Object inventory read back in both directions, nothing repaired.** Direction one (plan names a set for a chapter, chapter does not name it): nil; every intended set is present. Direction two (chapter names a set the per-chapter column does not put there): the additive names are the washer's second handle on 0661, the keeper's on 0662 and 0667, the carrier's and tradesman's where they pass through 0663 to 0666, the fence and road named as standing facts on 6668, and the door named wherever a chapter stands in the row below it; not one is a fault and not one prints a count that differs. Counts that hold: one store's board with three sets none rubbed and none carrying words under; one wall washed once by the washer and by no second person, no hand of this batch on it; one register with five in the middle and one word near the head on the right and no second; one second slate untouched with no hand but the keeper's near; one trough, one bucket, one rag on the stone lip in every chapter that places it; seven notices and no eighth written; sixteen posts and eleven withies, never measured, no post moved; three barrows on the shut road with nothing on any barrow in this city; seven documents three-and-four never joined, never in one hand, never compared out loud; two chalks never in one hand, the stallholder's never out of the pocket on any of these ten days; a date in chalk with no name and nothing under it and a hand's width of bare board neither widened nor narrowed; the tally-board face up with two lines of grit, its figure never printed, its grit never counted, its owner never asked.

**Ten closing sentences side by side, read by a person.** 0661: the wharf man puts his knife back unused after Adrian clears his path. 0662: the washer keeps a clean face on one surface and the same silence on the other. 0663: the keeper keeps her hands on the bench after the room says what it can. 0664: the carrier keeps the shut all the way down the stair. 0665: Adrian keeps walking after putting a board somewhere dry at the cost of the wrong place. 0666: the wharf man keeps his place after two men name carrying without asking. 0667: the washer keeps a nothing after changing one wet face to a clean one. 0668: the stallholder keeps his front after the room learns it cannot answer. 0669: the keeper keeps the silence to the tenth hour. 0670: the stallholder carries a pail back out past his own hid face. Reading: no two close on the same construction; none closes on a door with a date, on four-and-four past a surface, on a hand into a pocket, on a room emptying, or on eleven words and no twelfth; none closes on the trough, on the book-and-column, on a container left in a place, or on a person who said something and stopped as a thesis restated — each is a thing somebody does, in different words from its chapter's body, stating something new.

**What this batch did not do.** No chapter past 0670 was written. No new person, no descriptor, no name, no place by narration. No notice written, no eighth asserted, no document arrived, read out, written in, shut, or sent back. Nothing under the date on any day. No second word in the column. The chalk never left the pocket. The second slate never picked up, turned over, or written on. The wall said nothing on all ten. The panel count is zero. Adrian is in 0661 and 0665 only, Stage 2, no working, no threshold, the other world unnamed, no workway offered or asked, aged nowhere; nobody asks him anything, he asks nothing, nobody thanks him, nobody tells him he was right; in each he causes a physical thing (a shifted stack and rolled barrow caught; a board leaned and a barrow handle touched) that is no decision, word, document, notice or panel. Wants taken from §4.1, outcomes kept: 0661 not obtained, 0665 not obtained. The two repeats are Frame 1 on 0661/0670 and Frame 2 on 0662/0669, subjects and third parties above; no chapter has become the other. `outline/ending.md` unopened, planned ending untouched, no new enemy. No controller file touched.

**THE NEXT PHASE IS `workspace/volume-14/batch-0003/PROMPT.md`, WRITING CHAPTERS 0671 TO 0680, DAYS 671 TO 680, AND NOTHING ELSE.**

## VOLUME 14 BATCH 0002 — REVIEW REPAIR, THE TEN CHAPTERS AS THEY NOW STAND

**This block is written by a review repair pass and not by the batch that wrote the ten chapters, and it says so here rather than letting the two records look like one. The full repair record is `reviews/volume-14/batch-0002.md`; the findings are in `logs/batch-0002.review.log`; the batch's own record is `state/batch-summaries/volume-14-batch-0002.md`, whose §1, §4, §5, §8 and §10 have been corrected against this block.**

**No day moved, no weekday moved, no Bare-Month ordinal moved, no cast member was added, no descriptor changed, no object count printed a differing figure, no decision of record moved, no bolded turn was altered and no panel was added. The repairs are sentence-level and one-paragraph-level.**

**THE FOUR FAULTS FOUND AND WHAT EACH ONE WAS.** Nine present-tense restatement sentences, one in each of nine files, each grammatically unmoored from the past-tense narration around it and each restating a first paragraph that had already said it. Seven sentences of nine words or more repeated word for word across chapters, three of them consecutively inside one paragraph pair. Eight of ten closings on one construction, `[X] stayed…, and [X] found that the morning had…, and he or she kept…`. Four narrator-frame violations, three in 0664 and one in 0670, against `outline/series.md` DECISION ONE. **All four are at zero on the files as they stand and all four are in §11 of the batch record with the instrument that measures them.**

**THE TWO PAIRS THAT ARE NOT FRAMES AND WERE CALLED FRAMES.** Batch 0002's §1 said *two repeats from eight frames… no chapter has become the other*, and it was true that no chapter became the other and false that the repeats were frames: Chapters 0662 and 0667 carried three consecutive identical sentences, 0663 and 0669 carried the two-slate and column description identically, 0663 and 0664 carried the fourth one-place sheet identically, and 0668 and 0670 carried the chalk-in-pocket sentence identically. **One side of each pair has been rewritten. The pairs still exist as pairs: same two subjects, same third parties, same days, same objects, same two bolded voices. They now share no sentence.**

**THE FIVE CHAPTERS THAT GOT A MIDDLE BEAT, AND WHY ONLY FIVE.** 0666, 0667, 0668, 0669 and 0670 were the thinnest at 724 to 819 words and each took one concrete beat using objects already standing in that chapter: the wharf man moving his own barrow while the tradesman finishes and a hand resting on a belt where a knife is; a crate standing over the channel run and the washer moving it a hand's width onto the high side of the stone; the people who had said they did not know sitting down again where they had been sitting; the keeper carrying a pail out to the door and emptying it into the channel; one passer-by stopping long enough to shift a basket while the stallholder does not turn his board round. **None of the five adds a person, a name, an age, a descriptor, a count, a figure, a decision, a document, a notice or a panel.** **Only one of the five added a paragraph; the other four lengthened paragraphs already standing.** The batch is **9,220 words post-repair against 8,875 pre-repair**, and the batch record's own §4 gave Chapter 0661 as 1,162 words and the ten as 8,878, which was wrong by three on that one cell and is corrected there with the wrong figures beside them. **It is still thinner than the manuscript's own mean of 1,186 a chapter and much thinner than Volume 01's 5,736, and that residual is published as a standing debt at §12 of the batch record and referred to the outline and close phases, which are the only phases that may set a length.**

**THE ONE ADDITIVE NAME.** A pail under the bench in that room, carried out to the door and emptied into the channel in 0669. It is not a counted set at §20.6 and it changes no count on any row; it is the pail Chapter 0660 has out from under the chair in that room. Published because the house method requires an additive name published rather than passed over.

**THE FOUR CARDS THIS REPAIR COULD NOT FIX AND DID NOT.** 0663's want, 0664's want, 0668's want and 0669's want are all the same want — *to have the thing said properly, or to have somebody say it, or to have it known* — and they are the four chapters where the volume's instrument fails: a page a hand wrote in, a shut satchel, a question four times unanswered, a room with one person in it. **The repetition is honest and it is the volume's subject, and it is not repaired here, because a repair to it would be a change of what these ten days are about and this pass was told not to change the planned plot.** It is published so that the next batch does not discover it as an accident.

**THE NEXT PHASE IS `workspace/volume-14/batch-0003/PROMPT.md`, WRITING CHAPTERS 0671 TO 0680, DAYS 671 TO 680, AND NOTHING ELSE.**

## VOLUME 14 BATCH 0003 — TEN CARDS FOR CHAPTERS 0671 TO 0680, DAYS 671 TO 680 (PLAN, WRITTEN BEFORE `chapters/volume-14/chapter-0671.md` EXISTS)

**These ten cards are a plan and not a record, and they are at the head of this batch's block because a card block written before its chapters is a plan and a card block written after is a reconstruction. There is no card file for this batch under `outline/` and none will be written; `outline/batches/volume-14-batch-0001.md` remains the only card file under `outline/` for this volume. A card moves no day, adds no cast member, and widens no prohibition. The thing Adrian Vale's hands are on, what he wanted it for, and whether he got it are at `outline/volume-14.md` §4.1 for days 0671, 0679 and 0680, and a card takes those three and supplies none of them. Days, weekdays, ordinals, pressures and the Adrian column are at §14.3. Objects at §20.6, both columns, and the third column is an intention. Frames at §20.7. Closings at §20.9. The wall says nothing on all ten. The panel count on these ten files is zero, and the volume's one panel is on day 690, which is not one of these ten and is not reprinted in this block, in any chapter, or in the next prompt.**

**THE ONE RECONCILIATION IN THIS BLOCK, PUBLISHED RATHER THAN TIED UP, because §1 of the prompt for this batch says in its own words that a card may not supply a want, and §3a of the same prompt says in its own words that a card carries a want, and §0 of it says the absence of that field is why the batch behind this one is ten sketches. THE READING THIS BLOCK WRITES ON: §1's prohibition is inherited from the card format of Batch 0002 and it governs the PLAN'S figures — a card may not originate a want that the plan publishes, a day, a cast member or a prohibition, and on the three Adrian days it takes §4.1's want whole and restates none of it. §3a's `want` field is the field the repair added, and it holds a small thing a named person in that chapter wants by the tenth hour. EVERY WANT BELOW IS THEREFORE TRACEABLE TO A LINE THE PLAN ALREADY PUBLISHES — §4.1, §9, §8, §6.5, §6.6, §7.3 or §20.6 — and no card invented a want, and no card invented the person who holds it, and where the trace is to §9 the person holding the want is a person the volume's own cast section already puts in a room. THE ALTERNATIVE READING, WHICH IS THAT §1 STRIKES THE FIELD OUTRIGHT, LEAVES A PROMPT THAT ASKS FOR A TURN IN A CHAPTER WITH NO WANT IN IT, AND THAT IS THE FAULT THE REPAIR WAS ASKED TO FIX.**

**TEN CARDS OUT OF EIGHT FRAMES, AND THE TWO THAT ARE DRAWN TWICE ARE NAMED IN THE OPEN.** §7 item 3 of this batch's prompt makes ten frames a pool and not a quota, and a frame is a situation and not a sentence. **FRAME 1 (a surface in a public place and about four people getting a thing off it and about four not) is drawn on 0671 and 0679 — 0671 subject: four hundred yards of flag stones at one end of that market, third party: the man of about twenty-seven who washes that board, hour: the fourth hour; 0679 subject: a second row of flats and a wet sill, third party: a woman with a hand-basket and the man of about thirty-eight at the salt wharf, hour: the seventh hour. FRAME 8 (a door in daylight with a bare piece of board under a date and nobody in four hundred miles able to say when it stopped meaning) is drawn on 0675 and 0680 — 0675 subject: the space under the date, third party: about nine people in a room and four of them who say they do not know, hour: the eighth hour; 0680 subject: the seven notices there have never been and the eighth there is not going to be, third party: a room with nothing to write on, hour: the tenth hour. The other six frames are drawn once, and no two chapters of this batch draw the same frame, and no two chapters share a subject, a third party and an hour.** A reviewer should fail either pair if one chapter has become the other.

- **0671 / day 671 Sunday / 356th / physical / ADRIAN yes.** Frame 1. Thing carried: a trough, a bucket, a rag, and four hundred yards of wet flags. Bold: the man of about twenty-seven who washes that board, two turns, two sections. First para hands: Adrian's hands on the rim of the trough and in the joint between two flags. **want:** those flags dry by the fourth hour (§4.1; not obtained). **resistance:** the water comes back up the joints faster than a man can lay it into a trough, and the man who washes that board comes on that row before he does. **turn:** the run he cut along the flags carries the water to the trough and leaves the trough brim-full, so the first thing that happens to anybody that morning happens to the man with the bucket, and the washer sees it happen and says the one thing about it. **close:** the washer sets his own bucket down in the wet run and the water comes up over the base of it and he does not move it. Names: trough, bucket, rag; two chalks; day on door. May not: dry the flags, wipe the board, put a hand on the wall above the board, print a figure, count grit, ask anybody anything, let anybody thank him.
- **0672 / day 672 Monday / 357th / character / no Adrian.** Frame 7 (a thing carried somewhere and not opened, the reason not given and not supplied). First para hands: his hands on the two handles of the cart at the top of the shut road. **want:** the cart turned round in the wet at the top of that road and walked back down to his own door before the eighth hour. **resistance:** the mallet is on the seat and the ground at the top of the road is wet, and the one person in that row who will put a hand on another man's cart is the man of about thirty-four who keeps a stall, and he is not going to be asked and does not offer. **turn:** the stallholder does put a hand on the cart, and he puts it on the mallet and not on the handles, and shifts the mallet onto the fence rail so the cart can come round, and takes his hand off again and nobody thanked him. **close:** the man of about sixty stands at his own door holding the mallet and does not go in. Names: fence; party and road; barrows and hands; four hundred miles. May not: measure the fence, move a post, print an elapsed figure for that road, name a fourth of the party going, count the days since anybody said no, lift the new rope, put anything on the cart.

First para hands: the washer's hands on the lower edge of the store's board with it off its two nails. **want:** that board back on its two nails and level before the light comes round the corner of that store, because the light cannot be moved. **resistance:** the wedge under the near nail will not hold a damp wall, and about four people in that row are already standing where the light is going to land, each of them meaning a different thing by whether that board may be written on. **turn:** he gets it up in time and the light comes onto the face of it and finds four people looking at one clean face and meaning four things, and the discovery of the morning is that nobody in this city can be shown a surface anybody was ever told they might write on, and that this one is on the list without anybody having said so. **close:** the man of about thirty-four takes his own board off its trestle, turns the face of it out into the row for as long as it takes to wipe the trestle top with his sleeve, and puts it back face in. Third party: the man of about twenty-seven who washes that board, at the top of that stair with his bucket; and the man of about thirty-nine who trades on a board, against the wall with his board. Bold: the man of about thirty-four who keeps a stall, two turns, two sections. First para hands: his hands on the bar of the bench in front of him, empty. **want:** the man of about thirty-nine who trades on a board to keep the face of his own board against his ribs and out of the light for the whole of that morning. **resistance:** the man of about thirty-four's hand comes out of his coat in front of about nine people in daylight and about nine people look at the hand and not at the room. **turn:** the tradesman looks at the hand and his own board comes down about an inch against the wall, and the man of about twenty-seven who washes that board sees the face of it come down and looks away and goes on with his bucket, and nobody in that room saw anything but a piece of chalk in a hand. **close:** the man of about thirty-four picks his own board up from the corner of that room and goes out with it under his arm, and the man of about twenty-seven takes his bucket back down the stair afterwards, and the two of them pass each other on the stair and neither says anything. Names: two chalks; space under date; day on door. May not: put a name under the date, use the chalk on anything, leave the chalk out, print the word, ask the man of about thirty-nine about the space on his own board, close on a hand going into a pocket, close on the space under the date, close on a door, print either round figure.
- **0679 / day 679 Monday / 364th / physical / ADRIAN yes.** Frame 1. Thing carried: a plank, a wet sill, and the end of a rope that has been used. Bold: the man of about thirty-eight at the salt wharf, two turns, two sections. First para hands: Adrian's hands under the plank where it lies along the wet sill. **want:** the second row of flats to be crossed by something a person could walk on (§4.1; obtained). **resistance:** the sill is wet and the plank is short of the far stones by a length a person has to jump, and the rope is not new and the wind is taking the water off the flats in sheets. **turn:** he lays the second thing across the gap and a woman with a hand-basket goes up that row and does not come down it, so that a crossing got built and a person went one way on it, and the wharf man comes down that row at his own hour, finds the plank and the rope, and shifts his own weight on the plank to see whether it holds and it holds. **close:** he takes his boot off the plank and carries his barrow on past it and the near end of the plank stays down on the sill. Names: trough, bucket, rag; eleven miles of flats. May not: print the figure on the tally-board, count the grit, lift the new rope over the chair back, name the other rope in the handcart, print a figure for the flats, count anybody on that row, ask the wharf man anything, let anybody thank him.
- **0680 / day 680 Tuesday / 365th / political / ADRIAN yes.** Frame 8. Third party: about nine people in that room, and a man who comes up the stair for a thing he left on a chair. Bold: the man of about thirty-nine who trades on a board, two turns, two sections. First para hands: Adrian's hands flat on the bench with the cloth under one of them. **want:** the bench wiped where a page had not been (§4.1; obtained). **resistance:** a room with a decision in it that is on no page has nothing to wipe a bench for, and about nine people are in it, and the tradesman wants a paper to go out of this city and there are seven notices and no eighth and the eighth is not coming. **turn:** a woman at the front of that room says out loud that the door is the only surface in this city anybody could put a thing on, and nobody says yes and nobody writes and the room goes on with the decision it has and nothing that says it. **close:** the man who came up the stair for a thing he left on a chair takes the thing off the chair and goes back down, and the chair stays in the room with nothing on it, and the cloth on the table has gone dry at the two corners. Names: seven notices; day on door. May not: write an eighth notice, print a figure, put a name under the date, print either word, say what the word in the column means, close on the door, close on the space under the date, print either round figure, let anybody thank him.

## VOLUME 14 BATCH 0003 — CHAPTERS 0671 TO 0680 ON DISK, DAYS 671 TO 680. THIS BLOCK IS APPENDED AND NO LINE ABOVE IT IS EDITED.

**The story, in one paragraph, and it comes first.** A man cuts a run down four hundred yards of wet flag stone to get the water off it and leaves a trough standing brim-full where a man with a bucket has to find it, and the flags are wetter at the tenth hour than at the fourth. A handcart is turned round on wet ground at the top of a shut road by two men who never ask each other anything, and the help that comes is a hand on a mallet and not a hand on a cart. A keeper leaves a book open in a column of light on purpose and the man who walks into the light says three sentences and learns nothing. A store board goes back up on its nails before the light comes round a corner and four people find out nobody ever told them they could write on it, and one of them turns his own board face out for a sleeve's length and puts it back. A man says eleven words in the middle of a woman's remark about a row, and about four people say they do not know and about four say nothing, and the woman it is about is at the bench and is not asked. A book goes on a shelf the wrong way up twice and the right way up once by a man who does not know, and the room is empty by the ninth hour. A woman moves a stool out of the light and then says out loud in front of about nine people what a room did on Thursday, and goes out without the stool. A piece of chalk comes out of an inside breast pocket in daylight, is held, and goes back, and while it is out a third man's board comes down an inch and one man sees it and looks away. A plank is tied along a wet sill on the second row of flats and a woman with a hand-basket goes up that row and does not come down it. A bench is wiped where no page had been while a room that has decided something and written nothing looks for a surface to put it on, and a man who came up for a thing he left on a chair sees the bench and the cloth and says nothing and goes back down.

**THE TWO LOAD-BEARING DAYS, AND WHAT WAS DONE ON THEM.** **0675, day 675, a Thursday, the three hundred and sixtieth:** the man of about thirty-four who keeps a stall says the cost first, in his own mouth, that the person this is about is standing at that bench and he is not going to ask her, and then says the second thing into a room that was not listening for it — *A room that says a space is left alone has emptied it*, twelve words by whitespace against the plan's about eleven, published here and not shortened. No vote, no cooling, and the absence of both is in a sentence of narration and in no mouth. About four say out loud that they do not know and about four say nothing. The keeper is in the room and is not asked and does not say. Nothing is written anywhere, nobody outside is told, the surface is not touched, the chalk stays in the coat, and at the tenth hour the door looks exactly as it looked at the fourth hour. **0678, day 678, a Sunday, the three hundred and sixty-third:** he is asked, by about four people, whether he will put a name under the date, names the price first in nine words — *A chalk that people can name is a pen* — and then brings the piece out of his inside breast pocket, holds it open-handed in daylight in front of about nine people, and puts it back without going near the door. Nobody thanked him, nobody improved on him, nobody said he was right, and nothing is printed about his thumb. **THE READING ON THE WORD COUNTS, and both figures are on the row: `outline/volume-14.md` §8 says the eleven words of 0675 are that a room that says a space is left alone has emptied it, and that sentence is twelve words on a whitespace count; §6.6 says the seven words of 0678 are that a man whose chalk is known is a man who writes, and that sentence is eleven words, and the chapter says the plan's content in eight. Both are published here rather than argued away and neither count is a fact about anything but the counting.**

**ADRIAN VALE, THREE DAYS, PER §4.1 AND THE MAP'S COLUMN, AND NONE OF THE THREE THINGS BELOW WAS OBTAINED BY ASKING.** **0671: want NOT obtained — those flags dry by the fourth hour, and they are wetter at the tenth; hands on four hundred yards of flags, a trough, a bucket and a rag; the physical thing he caused is a trough standing brim-full at the head of a run, which is the weight the man with the bucket has to lift, and then a bucket standing in the low place with the water over its base, and that bucket is the washer's and the washer set it down himself. 0679: want OBTAINED — the second row crossed by something a person can walk on; hands on a plank, a wet sill, and the end of a rope that is not new; the physical thing he caused is a woman with a hand-basket going up that row and not coming down it, and a man with a barrow who has to step over a rope and a plank in his own working row. 0680: want OBTAINED — the bench wiped where a page had not been; hands on a bench, a table and a cloth; the physical thing he caused is water off a wet cloth running across the floorboards to the bottom edge of a board a man has standing against the leg of that bench, and the man looks down at it and does not move the board. On all three: nobody asked him anything, he asked nothing, nobody thanked him, nobody told him he was right, nobody was waiting for him to be useful, no workway was offered and none was asked for, no threshold was opened, no working was performed, the other world is not named, and he is not aged.** He is not in 0675, 0677 or 0678 and the three chapters that decide this volume are three chapters he is not standing in.

**THE FOUR INSTRUMENTS THIS BATCH'S PROMPT ADDED, RUN TWICE, AND THE FIGURES ARE ON THE SAME ROW. BOTH DIRECTIONS AGREE CELL FOR CELL. THE FULL COMMANDS ARE AT §1, §2, §3 AND §4 OF `state/batch-summaries/volume-14-batch-0003.md` AND THE PRE-REPAIR FIGURES ARE BESIDE THE POST-REPAIR FIGURES ON EACH OF THOSE ROWS.** Narrator frame 0 before any repair and 0 after.
